<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Annie and Bradley each think of a number. 

The difference between their numbers is $6$.

The sum of their numbers is $20$.

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number Annie thinks $= a$

Let the number Bradley thinks $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned} 
a + b      &= 20 \\\\
a - b      &= 6         
\end{aligned}
$

There are two variables, $a$ and $b$. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.

Subtract the second equation from the first to reduce it to one variable.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b - (a - b)     &= 20 - 6 \\\\
a + b -a + b        &= 14 \\\\
2b                  &= 14 \\\\
b                   &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
b                   &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

Let's substitute the value of $b$ in one of the initial equations.

$
\begin{aligned} 
a + b                          &= 20 \\\\
a + 7                          &= 20 \\\\
a                              &= 20 - 7 \\\\
a                              &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

Annie's number $= 13$

Bradley's number $= 7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7, 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number Annie thinks $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the number Bradley thinks $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
a + b      &= 20 \\\\
a - b      &= 6         
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
There are two variables, $a$ and $b$. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.
<emptyline>
Subtract the second equation from the first to reduce it to one variable.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b - (a - b)     &= 20 - 6 \\\\
a + b -a + b        &= 14 \\\\
2b                  &= 14 \\\\
b                   &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
b                   &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's substitute the value of $b$ in one of the initial equations.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
a + b                          &= 20 \\\\
a + 7                          &= 20 \\\\
a                              &= 20 - 7 \\\\
a                              &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Annie's number $= 13$
<emptyline>
Bradley's number $= 7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7, 13$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Alice thinks of a number.

Alice calls her number $a$.

**Alice adds $7$ to her number, and then doubles her answer**.

Write an expression, using $a$, to show what Alice does.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (7 + a) \times 2 \\\\
&= 14 + 2a
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14 + 2a$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= (7 + a) \times 2 \\\\
&= 14 + 2a
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14 + 2a$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jack thinks of a number.

Jack calls his number $n$.

**Jack multiplies his number by $3$, and then subtracts $5$**.

**He gets the answer $16$**.

Use this information to write down an equation, and then solve it to find $n$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3n - 5 &= 16 \\\\
3n     &= 16 + 5 \\\\
3n     &= 21 \\\\
n      &= \dfrac {21} {3} \\\\
n      &= \dfrac {7 \times 3} {3} \\\\
n      &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
n      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
3n - 5 &= 16 \\\\
3n     &= 16 + 5 \\\\
3n     &= 21 \\\\
n      &= \dfrac {21} {3} \\\\
n      &= \dfrac {7 \times 3} {3} \\\\
n      &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
n      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much carrot does he need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the carrot required = a

$
\begin{aligned}
a  &= \dfrac{1}{3} \times 600 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{1}{3} \times 200 \times 3 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{1}{\cancel 3} \times 200 \times \cancel 3 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= 200 \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$

Carrot required $= 200$ gm.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

200 gm

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much tomato does he need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the tomato needed = a

$
\begin{aligned}
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{1}{3} + \dfrac{1}{2} + \dfrac{1}{12})) \times 600 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{1 \times 4}{3 \times 4} + \dfrac{1 \times 6}{2 \times 6} + \dfrac{1 \times 1}{12 \times 1})) \times 600 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{4}{12} + \dfrac{6}{12} + \dfrac{1}{12})) \times 600 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{4 + 6 + 1}{12})) \times 600 \\\\
a    &= (1- \dfrac{11}{12})\times 600 \\\\
a    &= (\dfrac{1 \times 12}{1 \times 12}- \dfrac{11\times 1}{12 \times 1})\times 600 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{12 - 11}{12} \times 600 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{1}{12} \times 600 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{1}{12} \times 12 \times 50 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{1}{\cancel {12}} \times \cancel {12} \times 50 \\\\
a    &= 50 \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$

Tomatoes needed $= 50$ gm.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

50 gm

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the tomato needed = a
<emptyline>

a    &= (1-(\dfrac{4}{12} + \dfrac{6}{12} + \dfrac{1}{12})) \times 600 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{4 + 6 + 1}{12})) \times 600 \\\\
a    &= (1- \dfrac{11}{12})\times 600 \\\\
a    &= (\dfrac{1 \times 12}{1 \times 12}- \dfrac{11\times 1}{12 \times 1})\times 600 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{12 - 11}{12} \times 600 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{1}{12} \times 600 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{1}{12} \times 12 \times 50 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{1}{\cancel {12}} \times \cancel {12} \times 50 \\\\
a    &= 50 \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Tomatoes needed $= 50$ gm.

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2294EA9D-2E83-4996-BA7A-789DAF3604B5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$3 \ x$’s balance with $10 \ y$’s. 

If one $x$ weighs $1.5 \ \text{g}$, how much does one $y$ weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Weight of one $x = 1.5$

So,

$
\begin{aligned}
3x             &= 10y \\\\
3 \times 1.5   &= 10y \\\\
4.5            &= 10y \\\\
y              &= \dfrac{4.5}{10} \\\\
y              &= 0.45
\end{aligned}
$

Weight of one $y = 0.45$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.45$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
Weight of one $x = 1.5$
<emptyline>
So,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3x             &= 10y \\\\
3 \times 1.5   &= 10y \\\\
4.5            &= 10y \\\\
y              &= \dfrac{4.5}{10} \\\\
y              &= 0.45
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of one $y = 0.45$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$0.45$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7FF1BAEB-BDAB-4CEE-9553-05641321E1F6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Jack has thought of two numbers.

When he multiplies them together he gets $96$.

When he takes one number away from the other, he gets $4$.

What are the two numbers? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$a \times b = 96$

$a - b = 4$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $96$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $4$.

Factors of $96 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 96$

Factor pairs of $96$ that have difference of $4$ = (2, 6),(4,8),(8,12),(12,16).

| Factors   |  Difference  |    Product       |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------: |
| $2, 6$    |    $4$       |       12         |
| $4, 8$    |    $4$       |       32         |
| $8, 12$   |    $4$       |       96         |
| $12, 16$  |    $4$       |       192        |

A quick check shows that $8$ and $12$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 8,12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8, 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the second number $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$a \times b = 96$
<emptyline>
$a - b = 4$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.
<emptyline>
- The product of the $2$ numbers is $96$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $4$.
<emptyline>
Factors of $96 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 96$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $96$ that have difference of $4$ = (2, 6),(4,8),(8,12),(12,16).
<emptyline>
| Factors   |  Difference  |    Product       |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------: |
| $2, 6$    |    $4$       |       12         |
| $4, 8$    |    $4$       |       32         |
| $8, 12$   |    $4$       |       96         |
| $12, 16$  |    $4$       |       192        |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $8$ and $12$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. rtq_abbr_md_quadratic_note*
<emptyline>
The two numbers are $= 8,12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1DBEA292-BB97-4A3B-B433-7AC9B18147AD'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Rose is making a lemon cake. Part of the recipe for the cake mix is below.

$\dfrac{1}{4}$ of the cake mix is sugar

$\dfrac{1}{2}$ of the cake mix is flour

$\dfrac{1}{12}$ of the cake mix is lemons

The rest of the cake mix is made from other ingredients.

If there are $900 \ \text{grams}$ of cake mix

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOANSWER
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many grams of sugar are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the grams of sugar $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a  &= \dfrac{1}{4} \times 900 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{1}{4} \times 225 \times 4 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{1}{\cancel 4} \times 225 \times \cancel 4 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= 225 \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$

Carrot required $= 225$ gm.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

225 gm

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the grams of sugar $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a  &= \dfrac{1}{4} \times 900 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{1}{4} \times 225 \times 4 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{1}{\cancel 4} \times 225 \times \cancel 4 \text{gm} \\\\
a  &= 225 \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Carrot required $= 225$ gm.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>225 gm
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many grams of ingredients other than lemons, flour and sugar are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the other ingredients $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{1}{4} + \dfrac{1}{2} + \dfrac{1}{12})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{1 \times 3}{4 \times 3} + \dfrac{1 \times 6}{2 \times 6} + \dfrac{1 \times 1}{12 \times 1})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{3}{12} + \dfrac{6}{12} + \dfrac{1}{12})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{3 + 6 + 1}{12})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1- \dfrac{10}{12})\times 900 \\\\
a    &= (\dfrac{1 \times 12}{1 \times 12}- \dfrac{10\times 1}{12 \times 1})\times 900 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{12 - 10}{12} \times 900 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{2}{12} \times 900 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{2}{2 \times 6} \times 6 \times 150 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{\cancel 2}{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \times \cancel 6 \times 150 \\\\
a    &= 150 \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$

Other ingredients $= 150$ gm.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

150 gm

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the other ingredients $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{1}{4} + \dfrac{1}{2} + \dfrac{1}{12})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{1 \times 3}{4 \times 3} + \dfrac{1 \times 6}{2 \times 6} + \dfrac{1 \times 1}{12 \times 1})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{3}{12} + \dfrac{6}{12} + \dfrac{1}{12})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1-(\dfrac{3 + 6 + 1}{12})) \times 900 \\\\
a    &= (1- \dfrac{10}{12})\times 900 \\\\
a    &= (\dfrac{1 \times 12}{1 \times 12}- \dfrac{10\times 1}{12 \times 1})\times 900 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{12 - 10}{12} \times 900 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{2}{12} \times 900 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{2}{2 \times 6} \times 6 \times 150 \\\\
a    &= \dfrac{\cancel 2}{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \times \cancel 6 \times 150 \\\\
a    &= 150 \text{gm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Other ingredients $= 150$ gm.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>150 gm
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

At ‘Canty’s Candy Shop’ the following prices are charged.

$3$ Mars bars and $1$ Crunchie bar costs $\pounds 1.90$.

$1$ Mars bars and $1$ Crunchie bar costs $90 \ \text{pence}$.

</div>

<div class='working'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much will $2$ Mars bars cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of one Mars bars $= m$

Let the price of one Crunchie bars $= c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3m + 1c        &= \pounds 1.90 \\\\
1m + 1c        &= 90 \text{p} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

There are two variables, $m$ and $c$. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable. Subtract the second equation from the first to reduce it to one variable.


$
\begin{aligned} 
3m + 1c - (1m + 1c)   &= \pounds 1.90 - 90 \text{p} \\\\
3m + 1c - (1m + 1c)   &= 1.90 \times 100 \text{p} - 90 \text{p} \\\\
3m + 1c - (1m + 1c)   &= 190 - 90 \\\\
3m + 1c - 1m - 1c     &= 100 \\\\
2m                    &= 100 \\\\
m                     &= \dfrac{100}{2} \\\\
m                     &= \dfrac{50 \times 2}{2} \\\\
m                     &= \dfrac{50 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
m                     &= 50
\end{aligned}
$

Price of two mars bars $= 2 \times 50 = 100 $


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>



</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the price of one Mars bars $= m$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-5EDBB6FA-F0A3-4104-A880-4EE0FBC2B4DC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If two numbers multiply to give $36$ and their sum is $15$, what are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the two numbers $= a$ and $b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b          &= 36 \\\\
a + b               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.


Factors of $36 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18, 36$

| Numbers   |  Multiplication  |  Sum  |
|:-------:  |:---------------: |:----: |
| $1, 36$   | $36$             | $37$  |
| $2, 18$   | $36$             | $20$  |
| $3, 12$   | $36$             | $15$  |

A quick check shows that $3$ and $12$ meets all the criteria above.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3, 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the two numbers $= a$ and $b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b          &= 36 \\\\
a + b               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.
<emptyline>

Factors of $36 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18, 36$
<emptyline>
| Numbers   |  Multiplication  |  Sum  |
|:-------:  |:---------------: |:----: |
| $1, 36$   | $36$             | $37$  |
| $2, 18$   | $36$             | $20$  |
| $3, 12$   | $36$             | $15$  |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $3$ and $12$ meets all the criteria above.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3, 12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-64D4EF83-2942-4F33-A491-013A4EA9AB58'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In the last year a library bought $237$ new books and removed $67$ books. 

There were $5745$ books in the library at the end of the year.

How many books were in the library at the **start** of the year?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5745 - 237 + 67 \\\\
&= 5575 
\end{aligned}
$

Number of books at the start of the year $= 5575$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

5575

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5745 - 237 + 67 \\\\
&= 5575 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of books at the start of the year $= 5575$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>5575
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-BCB0259A-165B-499C-A83E-10AF2B1152C5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Kristina bought a bag of sweets and ate $\dfrac{3}{5}$  of them. If she ate $18$ sweets, how many sweets were left over?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{3}{5} \times a &= 18 \\\\
a                     &= \dfrac{18 \times 5}{3} \\\\
a                     &= \dfrac{6 \times 3 \times 5}{3} \\\\
a                     &= \dfrac{6 \times \cancel 3 \times 5}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a                     &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

Number of sweets left $= 30 - 18 = 12$

                      

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

12

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sweets $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{3}{5} \times a &= 18 \\\\
a                     &= \dfrac{18 \times 5}{3} \\\\
a                     &= \dfrac{6 \times 3 \times 5}{3} \\\\
a                     &= \dfrac{6 \times \cancel 3 \times 5}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a                     &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of sweets left $= 30 - 18 = 12$
<emptyline>
                      
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>12
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-35F0BC16-E836-43E9-8758-E1C8EC5DF410'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In a money-bag there are an equal number of $2$ pence and $5$ pence pieces and no other coins. How many coins are in the bag altogether if the total amount of money is $\pounds 1.26$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of coins for each pence $= x$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2x + 5x     &= \pounds 1.26 \\\\
7x          &= 1.26 \times 100 \text{p} \\\\
7x          &= 126 \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{126}{7} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{7 \times 18 }{7} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{\cancel 7 \times 18 }{\cancel7} \\\\
x           &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Number of coins altogether $2 \times 18 = 36 $

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of coins for each pence $= x$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2x + 5x     &= \pounds 1.26 \\\\
7x          &= 1.26 \times 100 \text{p} \\\\
7x          &= 126 \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{126}{7} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{7 \times 18 }{7} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{\cancel 7 \times 18 }{\cancel7} \\\\
x           &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of coins altogether $2 \times 18 = 36 $
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F858B554-4019-416C-AE6B-CC4D3E87412B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Oni has $11$ penpals. Last week she wrote to all of them.

She wrote a $4$-page letter to some of her penpals and a $3$-page letter to the rest. Altogether she wrote $38$ pages.

To how many penpals did Oni write a $3$-page letter ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of penpals whom Oni wrote 3 page letter $= a$

The number of penpals whom Oni wrote 4 page letter $= 11 - a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times a + 4 \times {(11 - a)}  &= 38 \\\\
3a + 4(11 - a)                    &= 38 \\\\
3a + 44 - 4a                      &= 38 \\\\
a                                 &= 44 - 38 \\\\
a                                 &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$

Number of penpals whom Oni wrote 3 page letter $= 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

6

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of penpals whom Oni wrote 3 page letter $= a$
<emptyline>
The number of penpals whom Oni wrote 4 page letter $= 11 - a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times a + 4 \times {(11 - a)}  &= 38 \\\\
3a + 4(11 - a)                    &= 38 \\\\
3a + 44 - 4a                      &= 38 \\\\
a                                 &= 44 - 38 \\\\
a                                 &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of penpals whom Oni wrote 3 page letter $= 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>6
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

If $a$ and $b$ are whole numbers, then $a \ \clubsuit \ b$ means $(b \times  b) \div (a + 1)$

So for example, $2 \ \clubsuit \ 6 = (6 \times 6) \div (2 + 1) = 36 \div 3 = 12$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $1 \ \clubsuit \ 10$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (10 \times 10) \div (1 + 1) \\\\
&= 100 \div (2) \\\\
&= \dfrac{100}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{50 \times 2}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{50 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
&= 50
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>

&= \dfrac{50 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
&= 50
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $(3 \ \clubsuit \ 4) \ \clubsuit \ 5$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (5 \times 5) \div (((4 \times 4) \div (3 + 1))+ 1) \\\\
&= (25) \div ((16 \div 4)+ 1) \\\\
&= (25) \div (\dfrac{16}{4}+ 1) \\\\
&= (25) \div (\dfrac{4 \times 4}{4}+ 1) \\\\
&= (25) \div (\dfrac{4 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4}+ 1) \\\\
&= 25 \div (4+1) \\\\
&= \dfrac{25}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{5 \times 5}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 5}{\cancel 5} \\\\
&= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>

&= 5
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $6  \ \clubsuit \  y = 7$, what is the value of $y$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(y \times y) \div (6 + 1) &= 7 \\\\
(y \times y) \div 7       &= 7 \\\\
y \times y                &= 7 \times 7 \\\\
y                         &= \sqrt{(7 \times 7)} \\\\
y                         &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

(y \times y) \div 7       &= 7 \\\\
y \times y                &= 7 \times 7 \\\\
y                         &= \sqrt{(7 \times 7)} \\\\
y                         &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Billy is given some toffees by his father. 
He eats one and then shares the rest out equally between himself and Emily. 
He then eats another and then shares the rest out equally between himself and Detti. 
He eats one more and gives the last one to Sean.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

<div class='working'>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many toffees did Detti get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of toffees $= a$

As per the question,

After eating $1$ and dividing it equally with emily, toffees left $= b = \dfrac{(a-1)}{2}$

Again after eating one and dividing it equally with Detti, toffees left $= c = \dfrac{b-1}{2}$

After eating one and giving last to Sean, toffees left $= c - 1 -1 = 0$

So,

$
\begin{aligned}
c - 1 -1 &= 0 \\\\
c        &= 2 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Number of toffees detti got $= 2$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>

Number of toffees Emily got $= b$

$

b              &= 4 + 1 \\\\
b              &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

Number of toffees Emily got $= 5$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-017292F5-B2A1-4C79-85DD-F6BA7E5EF275'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Three pet rabbits cost $\pounds 19.70$.

The second rabbit cost $\pounds 2$ more than the first.

The third rabbit cost $80 \text{p}$ less than the second. 

What is the cost of the first rabbit?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of first rabbit $= r$

Cost of second rabbit $= 2 + r$

Cost of third rabbit $= (2 + r) - 0.8$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
r + 2 + r + (2 + r) - 0.8 &= 19.70 \\\\
3 r + 4 - 0.8             &= 19.70 \\\\
3 r + 3.2                 &= 19.70 \\\\
3 r                       &= 19.70 - 3.2 \\\\
3 r                       &= 16.50 \\\\
r                         &= \dfrac {16.50} {3} \\\\
r                         &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 3} {3} \\\\
r                         &= \dfrac {5.5 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
r                         &= \pounds 5.5
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of first rabbit $=\pounds 5.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 5.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of first rabbit $= r$
<emptyline>
Cost of second rabbit $= 2 + r$
<emptyline>
Cost of third rabbit $= (2 + r) - 0.8$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
r + 2 + r + (2 + r) - 0.8 &= 19.70 \\\\
3 r + 4 - 0.8             &= 19.70 \\\\
3 r + 3.2                 &= 19.70 \\\\
3 r                       &= 19.70 - 3.2 \\\\
3 r                       &= 16.50 \\\\
r                         &= \dfrac {16.50} {3} \\\\
r                         &= \dfrac {5.5 \times 3} {3} \\\\
r                         &= \dfrac {5.5 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
r                         &= \pounds 5.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of first rabbit $=\pounds 5.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 5.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8CA44EE7-F16E-4F90-964D-A9A239631F65'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>


On her birthday last year, Emily was $140\ \text{cm}$ tall. When she measured herself on her birthday today, she calculated that she had grown by a fifth of the height she was a year ago.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOANSWER
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How tall is she now?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (1 + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140  \\\\
&= (\dfrac{1 \times 5}{1 \times 5} + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{5 + 1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
&= \dfrac{6}{5} \times 140  \\\\
&= \dfrac{6}{5} \times 28 \times 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{6}{\cancel 5} \times 28 \times \cancel 5 \\\\
&= 28 \times 6 \\\\
&= 168 \text{ cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$168 \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= (1 + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140  \\\\
&= (\dfrac{1 \times 5}{1 \times 5} + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{5 + 1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
&= \dfrac{6}{5} \times 140  \\\\
&= \dfrac{6}{5} \times 28 \times 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{6}{\cancel 5} \times 28 \times \cancel 5 \\\\
&= 28 \times 6 \\\\
&= 168 \text{ cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$168 \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Emily's brother, Jack, is now $105\ \text{cm}$ tall, having grown by one sixth of his height a year ago. How tall was Jack one year ago?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the age $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
(1 + \dfrac{1}{6}) \times a                                       &= 105 \\\\
(\dfrac{1 \times 6}{1 \times 6} + \dfrac{1}{6}) \times a          &= 105 \\\\
(\dfrac{6 + 1}{6}) \times  a                                      &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{7}{6} \times a                                             &= 105 \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{105 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times 7 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel 7 \times 6}{\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= 90 
\end{aligned}
$

His age a year ago $= 90$ cm

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the age $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(1 + \dfrac{1}{6}) \times a                                       &= 105 \\\\
(\dfrac{1 \times 6}{1 \times 6} + \dfrac{1}{6}) \times a          &= 105 \\\\
(\dfrac{6 + 1}{6}) \times  a                                      &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{7}{6} \times a                                             &= 105 \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{105 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times 7 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel 7 \times 6}{\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= 90 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
His age a year ago $= 90$ cm
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$90$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-AEF8D987-2111-4062-8FFB-061D2FAA0A7F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Amir, Anna and Arjun have bought some fruit at a market stall.

Amir bought $2$ Pears and $1$ Mango for $\pounds 1.35$

Anna bought $1$ Pear and $2$ Mangos for $\pounds 1.20$

Arjun bought $1$ Pear, $1$ Mango and $1$ Apple for $\pounds 1$

How much, in pence, did the apple cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of pear $= p$

Let the price of Mango $= m$

Let the price of apple $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2p + m    &= 1.35 \\\\
p + 2m    &= 1.20 \\\\
p + m + a &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

For calculating the cost of apple we will have to use elimination method. So lets add eqaution 1 and equation 2 first

$
\begin{aligned}
2p + m + p + 2m   &= 1.35 + 1.20 \\\\
3p + 3m           &= 2.55 \\\\
3(p + m)          &= 2.55 \\\\
p + m             &= \dfrac{2.55}{3} \\\\
p + m             &= 0.85
\end{aligned}
$

Now we have,

$
\begin{aligned}
p + m + a         &= 1 \\\\
p + m             &= 0.85 
\end{aligned}
$

So now, using elimination method on the above two equations

$
\begin{aligned}
p + m + a - (p + m) &= 1 - 0.85 \\\\
p + m + a - p - m   &= 1 - 0.85 \\\\
a                   &= \pounds 0.15 \\\\
a                   &= 0.15 \times 100 \text{p} \\\\ 
a                   &= 15 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the cost of an apple $= 15$ p


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DAFC965B-0C09-46A4-9759-E2AD3E3F5A28'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Dominic, Hannahand Abdul have $36$ sweets between them.

Dominic has half as many sweets as Hannah.

Abdul has $3$ times as many sweets as Hannah.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


How many sweets does Abdul have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets with Hannah $= a$.

Then the number of sweets with Dominic $= \dfrac{a}{2}$.

The number of sweets with Abdul $= 3a$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + \dfrac{a}{2} + 3a                                  &= 36 \\\\
4a +\dfrac{a}{2}                                       &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{4a \times 2}{1 \times 2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}         &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a}{2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}                           &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a + a}{2}                                      &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{9a}{2}                                          &= 36 \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{36 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{9\times 4 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{\cancel 9\times 4 \times 2}{\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                                      &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

Number of sweets Abdul had $= 3 \times 8 = 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sweets with Hannah $= a$.
<emptyline>
Then the number of sweets with Dominic $= \dfrac{a}{2}$.
<emptyline>
The number of sweets with Abdul $= 3a$.
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + \dfrac{a}{2} + 3a                                  &= 36 \\\\
4a +\dfrac{a}{2}                                       &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{4a \times 2}{1 \times 2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}         &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a}{2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}                           &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a + a}{2}                                      &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{9a}{2}                                          &= 36 \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{36 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{9\times 4 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{\cancel 9\times 4 \times 2}{\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                                      &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of sweets Abdul had $= 3 \times 8 = 24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

If $a$ and $b$ are numbers, then $a \ \hearts \ b$ means **'multiply $a$ by itself and then subtract $b$'**.

For example

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \hearts \  3 &= 2 \times 2 - 3 \\
                 &= 4 - 3 \\
                 &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $a$ when $a \ \hearts \ 4 = 32$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a - 4    &= 32 \\\\
a \times a        &= 32 + 4 \\\\
a \times a        &= 36 \\\\
a                 &= sqrt{36} \\\\
a                 &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a - 4    &= 32 \\\\
a \times a        &= 32 + 4 \\\\
a \times a        &= 36 \\\\
a                 &= sqrt{36} \\\\
a                 &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $x$ when $x \ \hearts \ x = 72$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
x \times x - x         &= 72 \\\\
x \times (x - 1)       &= 72 
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $72$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $1$.

Factors of $72 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9, 12, 18, 24, 36, 72$

Factor pairs of $72$ that have difference of $1$ = (1, 2),(2, 3),(3, 4),(8 ,9).

| Factors   |  Difference  |    Product       |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------: |
| $1, 2$    |    $1$       |       2          |
| $2, 3$    |    $1$       |       6          |
| $3, 4$    |    $1$       |       12         |
| $8, 9$    |    $1$       |       72         |

A quick check shows that $8$ and $9$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 8, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
x \times x - x         &= 72 \\\\
x \times (x - 1)       &= 72 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-39086DA3-F713-45C2-91F7-217AC5ABF006'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

You multiply a number by itself. You then multiply the new number by three. The answer is $192$.

What is your starting number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a \times a) \times 3 &= 192 \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {192} {3} \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {64 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {64 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= 64 \\\\
a                     &= \sqrt 64 \\\\
a                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

Starting number $=8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $=a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(a \times a) \times 3 &= 192 \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {192} {3} \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {64 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {64 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a ^ 2                 &= 64 \\\\
a                     &= \sqrt 64 \\\\
a                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Starting number $=8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DCF1FE2D-8937-40A2-8F2F-5103646889A4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A box contains $48$ pieces of fruit. Five eighths of them are apples and the rest of them are pears. How many pears are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of apples} &= \dfrac {5} {8} \times 48 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 48} {8} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
                        &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
                        &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pears}  &= 48 - 30 \\\\
                        &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of apples} &= \dfrac {5} {8} \times 48 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 48} {8} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
                        &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
                        &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pears}  &= 48 - 30 \\\\
                        &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-6B4DB8FD-6576-491E-B677-B12E7FA822F0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


Brendan and Sophie got $\pounds 0.20$ change when they paid with $\pounds 5$ for some tomatoes, green peppers and cucumbers which they bought at a farm stand.

They bought $5$ tomatoes, which weighed $2 \ \text{pounds}$ altogether. 

They bought two more tomatoes than green peppers.

How many cucumbers did they buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of cucumbers $= a$

Number of green peppers $= 5 - 2 = 3$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1.50 \times 2 + 40\text{p} \times 3 + 30 \text{p} \times a              &= \pounds 5 - \pounds 0.20 \\\\
1.50\times 100 \text{p} \times 2 + 40\text{p} \times 3 + 30 \text{p} \times a   &= 5\times 100 \text{p} - 0.20 \times 100 \text{p} \\\\
150 \times 2 + 40 \times 3 + 30  \times a                                       &= 500 - 20 \\\\
300 + 120 + 30a                                                                 &= 480 \\\\
420 + 30a                                                                       &= 480 \\\\
30a                                                                             &= 480 - 420 \\\\
30a                                                                             &= 60 \\\\
a                                                                               &= \dfrac{60}{30} \\\\
a                                                                               &= \dfrac{30 \times 2}{30} \\\\
a                                                                               &= \dfrac{\cancel {30} \times 2}{\cancel {30}} \\\\
a                                                                               &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

Number of cucumbers $= 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of cucumbers $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of green peppers $= 5 - 2 = 3$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1.50 \times 2 + 40\text{p} \times 3 + 30 \text{p} \times a              &= \pounds 5 - \pounds 0.20 \\\\
1.50\times 100 \text{p} \times 2 + 40\text{p} \times 3 + 30 \text{p} \times a   &= 5\times 100 \text{p} - 0.20 \times 100 \text{p} \\\\
150 \times 2 + 40 \times 3 + 30  \times a                                       &= 500 - 20 \\\\
300 + 120 + 30a                                                                 &= 480 \\\\
420 + 30a                                                                       &= 480 \\\\
30a                                                                             &= 480 - 420 \\\\
30a                                                                             &= 60 \\\\
a                                                                               &= \dfrac{60}{30} \\\\
a                                                                               &= \dfrac{30 \times 2}{30} \\\\
a                                                                               &= \dfrac{\cancel {30} \times 2}{\cancel {30}} \\\\
a                                                                               &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of cucumbers $= 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4006083B-3E90-419F-BA56-2A524560C84C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A bus started off from a bus station with $24$ people.

At  the the first stop $4$ people got off and some people got on.

At the second stop,no one got off but $3$ people got on.

There were then $35$ people.

How many people got on at the first stop?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of people got on $= p$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
24 - 4 + x + 3 &= 35 \\\\
x + 23         &= 35 \\\\
x              &= 35 - 23 \\\\
x              &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

Number of people got on $=12$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of people got on $= p$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
24 - 4 + x + 3 &= 35 \\\\
x + 23         &= 35 \\\\
x              &= 35 - 23 \\\\
x              &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of people got on $=12$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-BDA4A8AF-88B3-4D7B-82FC-E14471C0ED58'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The Jones children take their dogs for a walk.

There are $3$ times as many dogs as children.

The total number of legs is $56$. How many Jones children are then?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of children $= c$

Number of dogs $= 3 c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times c + 4 \times 3 c &= 56 \\\\
2 c + 12 c                &= 56 \\\\
14 c                      &= 56 \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {56} {14} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {14 \times 4} {14} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {14} \times 4} {\cancel {14}} \\\\
c                         &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Jone's children $=4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of children $= c$
<emptyline>
Number of dogs $= 3 c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times c + 4 \times 3 c &= 56 \\\\
2 c + 12 c                &= 56 \\\\
14 c                      &= 56 \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {56} {14} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {14 \times 4} {14} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {14} \times 4} {\cancel {14}} \\\\
c                         &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Jone's children $=4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='review'>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-186AAEE8-D55E-48C5-996E-8F8A69BD4AFB'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

On the planet Nodnol, the natives have a special sort of arithmetic using the symbol $\spadesuit$.

$3 \ \spadesuit \  4$ means add $3$ and $4$ and then add on the product of $3$ and $4$, so 

$3 \ \spadesuit \ 4 = 3 + 4 + (3 \times 4 ) = 19$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the value of $5 \ \spadesuit \  7$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5 + 7 + (5 \times 7) \\\\
&= 12 + 35 \\\\
&= 47
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$47$

</div>
</div>

&= 5 + 7 + (5 \times 7) \\\\
&= 12 + 35 \\\\
&= 47
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the value of $2 \ \spadesuit \ \dfrac{1}{2}$


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 + \dfrac{1}{2} + (2 \times \dfrac{1}{2}) \\\\
&= 2 + \dfrac{1}{2} + (\cancel 2 \times \dfrac{1}{\cancel 2}) \\\\
&= 2 + \dfrac{1}{2} + 1 \\\\
&= 3 + \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 2}{1 \times 2} + \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{6 + 1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{7}{2} \\\\
&= 3.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 + \dfrac{1}{2} + (2 \times \dfrac{1}{2}) \\\\
&= 2 + \dfrac{1}{2} + (\cancel 2 \times \dfrac{1}{\cancel 2}) \\\\
&= 2 + \dfrac{1}{2} + 1 \\\\
&= 3 + \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 \times 2}{1 \times 2} + \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{6 + 1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{7}{2} \\\\
&= 3.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $x \ \spadesuit \ 2 = 23$, what number is $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 2 + (x \times 2)   &= 23 \\\\
x + 2 + 2x             &= 23 \\\\
3x + 2                 &= 23 \\\\
3x                     &= 23 - 2 \\\\
3x                     &= 21 \\\\
x                      &= \dfrac{21}{3} \\\\
x                      &= \dfrac{3 \times 7}{3} \\\\
x                      &= \dfrac{\cancel 3 \times 7}{\cancel 3} \\\\
x                      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

3x + 2                 &= 23 \\\\
3x                     &= 23 - 2 \\\\
3x                     &= 21 \\\\
x                      &= \dfrac{21}{3} \\\\
x                      &= \dfrac{3 \times 7}{3} \\\\
x                      &= \dfrac{\cancel 3 \times 7}{\cancel 3} \\\\
x                      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $n \ \spadesuit \ n = 99$, what number is $n$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
n + n + (n \times n) &= 99 \\\\
2n + n \times n      &= 99 \\\\
n \times (n + 2)     &= 99
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $99$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $2$.

Factors of $99 = 1, 3, 9, 11, 33 \text{ and } 99$

Factor pairs of $99$ that have difference of $2$ = (9,11).

Therefore, only $9$ and $11$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 9,11$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9, 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
n + n + (n \times n) &= 99 \\\\
2n + n \times n      &= 99 \\\\
n \times (n + 2)     &= 99
\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-80AC4610-095A-4D6A-AD0D-BC985289FC32'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A bat ate $1050$ mosquitoes in four nights. Each night she ate $25$ more than the night before. 
How many did she eat on the first night? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of mosquitoes eaten on first day $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + (a + 25) + ((a + 25) + 25) + (((a + 25) + 25) + 25) &= 1050 \\\\
a + a + 25 + a + 25 + 25 + a + 25 + 25 + 25             &= 1050 \\\\
4a + 150                                                &= 1050 \\\\
4a                                                      &= 1050 - 150 \\\\
4a                                                      &= 900 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{900}{4} \\\\  
a                                                       &= \dfrac{225 \times 4}{4} \\\\  
a                                                       &= \dfrac{225 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\ 
a                                                       &= 225
\end{aligned}
$

Number of mosquitoes eaten on first day $= 225$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-71244B72-5BA2-4E9F-B6E1-473CCA0B319A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If two numbers multiply to give $36$ and their difference is $5$, what are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the two numbers $= a$ and $b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b          &= 36 \\\\
a - b                 &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.


Factors of $36 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18, 36$

| Numbers   |  Multiplication  |  Difference  |
|:-------:  |:----------------:|:------------:|
| $36, 1$   | $36$             | $35$         |
| $18, 2$   | $36$             | $16$         |
| $12, 3$   | $36$             | $9$          |
| $9, 4$    | $36$             | $5$          |


A quick check shows that $9$ and $4$ meets the criteria above.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the two numbers $= a$ and $b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b          &= 36 \\\\
a - b                 &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.
<emptyline>

Factors of $36 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18, 36$
<emptyline>
| Numbers   |  Multiplication  |  Difference  |
|:-------:  |:----------------:|:------------:|
| $36, 1$   | $36$             | $35$         |
| $18, 2$   | $36$             | $16$         |
| $12, 3$   | $36$             | $9$          |
| $9, 4$    | $36$             | $5$          |
<emptyline>

A quick check shows that $9$ and $4$ meets the criteria above.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4, 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C3254CDA-4FF0-4DD2-B767-58165F53A978'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Tasha thinks of a number, multiplies it by $3$ and then adds $8$. 
Her answer is $35$. What is the number she first thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question

$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 8   &= 35 \\\\
3a       &= 35 - 8 \\\\
3a       &= 27 \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {27} {3} \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {3 \times 9} {3} \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 9} {\cancel {3} } \\\\
a        &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The number $=9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 8   &= 35 \\\\
3a       &= 35 - 8 \\\\
3a       &= 27 \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {27} {3} \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {3 \times 9} {3} \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 9} {\cancel {3} } \\\\
a        &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $=9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-FC29A605-E71B-4E7D-B47B-150F54FA1D82'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Kristina and Ravi shared a box of sweets. 
Kristina ate $\dfrac{3}{5}$ of them. 

If $12$ sweets were left for Ravi, how many sweets were 
there in the box at the start?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets in the box $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(1 - \dfrac{3}{5}) \times a                                &= 12 \\\\
(\dfrac{1 \times 5}{1 \times 5} - \dfrac{3}{5}) \times a   &= 12 \\\\
(\dfrac{5 - 3}{5}) \times a                                &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac{2}{5} \times a                                      &= 12 \\\\
a                                                          &= \dfrac{12 \times 5}{2} \\\\
a                                                          &= \dfrac{2 \times 6 \times 5}{2} \\\\
a                                                          &= \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times 6 \times 5}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                                          &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

Nimita wants to hang some balloons for her birthday party. 
Each balloon needs $35 \ \text{cm}$ of string attached to it.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the largest number of balloons she can hang with a $4 \ \text{metre}$ ball of string?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the largest number of balloons $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a       &= \dfrac{4 \text{ m}}{35 \text{ cm}} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{400 \text{ cm}}{35 \text { cm}} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{5 \times 80}{7 \times 5} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{\cancel 5 \times 80}{7 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{80}{7} \\\\
a       &= 11.43       
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore largesr number of balloons $= 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the largest number of balloons $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a       &= \dfrac{4 \text{ m}}{35 \text{ cm}} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{400 \text{ cm}}{35 \text { cm}} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{5 \times 80}{7 \times 5} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{\cancel 5 \times 80}{7 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac{80}{7} \\\\
a       &= 11.43       
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore largesr number of balloons $= 11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What length of string would be left over? Give your answer in centimetres.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (11.43 - 11) \times 35 \\\\
&= 0.43 \times 35 \\\\
&= 15.05 \text{ cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$15.05$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

He is paid $\pounds 3.60$ per hour. How much does he earn in one week?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{330}{60} \text{hour} \times 3.60 \\\\
&= \dfrac{33 \times 10}{6 \times 10}  \times 6 \times 0.6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{33 \times \cancel {10}}{\cancel 6 \times \cancel {10}} \times \cancel 6 \times 0.6 \\\\
&= \pounds 19.8 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 19.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{330}{60} \text{hour} \times 3.60 \\\\
&= \dfrac{33 \times 10}{6 \times 10}  \times 6 \times 0.6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{33 \times \cancel {10}}{\cancel 6 \times \cancel {10}} \times \cancel 6 \times 0.6 \\\\
&= \pounds 19.8 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 19.8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1D99F148-94EA-46DC-A433-FB23AEAA80D8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Sharpay is going shopping for some new stationery.

She can buy three pencils and a ruler for $50 \text{p}$ or two pencils and two rulers for $56 \text{p}$. 

How much does each pencil cost her?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of a pencil $= p$

Let the cost of a ruler $= r$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned} 
3p + r      &= 50p \\\\
2p + 2r     &= 56p         
\end{aligned}
$

There are two variables, $p$ and $r$. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable. Multiply the first equation by $2$ to eliminate $r$.

$
\begin{aligned} 
3p + r                      &= 50 \\\\
2 \times 3p + 2 \times r    &= 2 \times 50 \\\\
6p + 2r                     &= 100
\end{aligned}
$

Subtract the second equation from the first to reduce it to one variable.

$
\begin{aligned}
6p + 2r                     &= 100 \\\\
2p + 2r                     &= 56 \\\\
6p + 2r - (2p + 2r)         &= 100 - 56 \\\\
6p + 2r - 2p - 2r           &= 44 \\\\
4p                          &= 44 \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac{44}{4} \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac{11 \times 4}{4} \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac{11 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
p                           &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of a pencil$= 11 \text{p}$. 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of a pencil $= p$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of a ruler $= r$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
3p + r      &= 50p \\\\
2p + 2r     &= 56p         
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

2 \times 3p + 2 \times r    &= 2 \times 50 \\\\
6p + 2r                     &= 100
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Subtract the second equation from the first to reduce it to one variable.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
6p + 2r                     &= 100 \\\\
2p + 2r                     &= 56 \\\\
6p + 2r - (2p + 2r)         &= 100 - 56 \\\\
6p + 2r - 2p - 2r           &= 44 \\\\
4p                          &= 44 \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac{44}{4} \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac{11 \times 4}{4} \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac{11 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
p                           &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of a pencil$= 11 \text{p}$. 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A3B8C55E-C07A-4847-8A7E-0AAC0B73B05E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Alice has saved $\pounds 10$ more than Becky. 

Becky has saved $\pounds 3$ more than Charlie.

Altogether Alice, Becky and Charlie have saved $\pounds 26.50$.


How much has Alice saved?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the saving of Becky $= a$

Savings of Alice $= a + 10$

Savings of Charlie $= a - 3$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 10 + a - 3   &= \pounds 26.50 \\\\
3a + 7               &= 26.50 \\\\
3a                   &= 19.5 \\\\
a                    &= \dfrac{3 \times 6.5}{3} \\\\
a                    &= \dfrac{\cancel 3 \times 6.5}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a                    &= 6.5
\end{aligned}
$ 

Alice savings $= 6.5 + 10 = 16.5 $

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the saving of Becky $= a$
<emptyline>
Savings of Alice $= a + 10$
<emptyline>
Savings of Charlie $= a - 3$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 10 + a - 3   &= \pounds 26.50 \\\\
3a + 7               &= 26.50 \\\\
3a                   &= 19.5 \\\\
a                    &= \dfrac{3 \times 6.5}{3} \\\\
a                    &= \dfrac{\cancel 3 \times 6.5}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a                    &= 6.5
\end{aligned}
$ 
<emptyline>
Alice savings $= 6.5 + 10 = 16.5 $
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$16.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out $\dfrac{1}{2} * \dfrac{1}{2}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (\dfrac{1}{2} + \dfrac{1}{2}) \times \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= (\dfrac{1 + 1}{2} )\times \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2}{2} \times \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \times \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{1}{2}$

</div>
</div>

&= (\dfrac{1 + 1}{2} )\times \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2}{2} \times \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \times \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $a$ when $a*2 = 30$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 2) \times 2 &= 30 \\\\
a + 2            &= \dfrac{30}{2} \\\\
a + 2            &= \dfrac{15 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a + 2            &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a + 2            &= 15 \\\\
a                &= 15 - 2 \\\\
a                &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

a + 2            &= \dfrac{30}{2} \\\\
a + 2            &= \dfrac{15 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a + 2            &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a + 2            &= 15 \\\\
a                &= 15 - 2 \\\\
a                &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $a$ when $a*a = 50$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
(a + a) \times a   &= 50 \\\\
2a \times a        &= 50 \\\\
a \times a         &= \dfrac{50}{2} \\\\
a \times a         &= \dfrac{25 \times 2}{2} \\\\  
a \times a         &= \dfrac{25 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\ 
a \times a         &= 25 \\\\
a                  &= \sqrt{25} \\\\
a                  &= 5              
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>

a \times a         &= \dfrac{25 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\ 
a \times a         &= 25 \\\\
a                  &= \sqrt{25} \\\\
a                  &= 5              
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
